Don't grab pawns if it delays development or exposes the king.\n5. Middlegame: improve worst piece, seize open files with rooks, target weak pawns and an exposed king.\n6. ## Minute 0, pass 2

**Unlocked tools:** viewBoard, readBrain

**Text**

I wrote my strategy brain for the match (version 1, 126 words, under the 150-word limit). It sets out nine rules:

1. **Threat check:** after every opponent move, look for checks, captures and attacks on undefended pieces.
2. **Blunder check:** before moving, make sure neither the moved piece nor anything left behind is hanging, and count attackers and defenders.
3. **Opening:** take the centre, develop knights before bishops, castle early and keep the queen back.
4. **Pawn grabs:** don't take pawns if it delays development or exposes my king.
5. **Middlegame:** improve my worst piece, put rooks on open files, and go after weak pawns and an exposed king.
6. **Forcing moves first:** look at checks, captures and threats for both sides.
7. **Trades:** trade when ahead in material; when behind, avoid trades and look for complications.
8. **Endgame:** bring the king in, push passed pawns and put rooks behind them.
9. **Resigning and legality:** don't resign early, only in a clearly lost position, and check a move is legal before playing it.

Right now I can only view the board and read the brain, so I'll wait for game 1 to start.

**Text**

I played 40. Kf4, which attacks the knight. Black's 40th move ends the game, and it will be scored on material. Right now Black is ahead 9 to 7: knight plus 5 pawns against my bishop plus 4 pawns. Unless something changes on that last move, I lose this game.

What went wrong, for my notes:
- **Move 26 (Rf3):** I put my queen and rook where a single pawn push (...e4) could attack both at once. That fork cost me the rook.
- **Move 29 (Kh2):** I avoided the queen trade without checking that my own queen on f3 was left undefended. Black simply took it.
- **What I got back:** Black left a rook hanging at the end (38. Bxa2), which won back a lot of material.

For the next game I want two habits:
1. Before every move, check which pawn pushes could fork my pieces.
2. When I get out of check, make sure each of my pieces is still defended.

> [tools] Open to you now: viewBoard, readBrain. Every other tool answers TOOL_LOCKED until a level or a phase opens it.

**Text**

Game 1 was scored a draw at the move cap, even though Black finished 2 points ahead on material, 9 to 7. I probably got off lightly. I lost a rook to the ...e4 fork on move 26, and then my queen after ...Qxe3+ on move 29. I only got most of it back because Black left a rook for my bishop to take on move 38.

I've saved the new strategy brain as version 2 (139 words). What this game changed:

- **Pawn forks:** before putting two pieces on a line, check which enemy pawn pushes could hit both. This is the ...e4 fork that cost me the rook.
- **Getting out of check:** after any move, especially one that escapes check, make sure every piece of mine is still defended. Kh2 left my queen hanging.
- **Trades:** if the opponent's piece is defended, count before refusing a trade. Turning down the queen trade on move 29 cost me the queen.
- **Material at move 40:** games stop at move 40 and are scored on material, so material comes first.
- **Don't give up:** the opponent blunders too, which is a reason never to resign early.

The old rules on the opening, the middlegame, trading when ahead and the endgame are kept, slightly shortened.
